and it is great for tasks such as classification for a linear layer you define the amount of input neurons and also the amount of output neurons this then means the linear layer can learn the relationship between the input neurons and the output neurons so if you have a linear layer for example that takes a bunch of inputs so a lot of neurons as inputs it can Bo that down to maybe only three emotions and output one of those three emotions the next layer we'll work with is called conf 1D and this is a convolutional layer it is great for learning patterns in sequential data such as sequences while linear layers are better at detecting global patterns in data so these layers will contain neurons that we'll be able to train the next category of layers is called functional layers all the negative numbers and make those zero so it'll be nonlinear now the next layer is called the Dropout layer and this layer prevents overfitting in the network by randomly turning off neurons this forces the network to not rely on specific features and that can help prevent overfitting so let's say you're studying for an exam in the real world so if you are reading a book and some of the textbook pages are randomly hidden then it'll force you to learn material from multiple sources and not just from a single page which you'll have to remember so this means you won't be able to just remember a single page and thereby your overall understanding will be even better so that is kind of the same concept we're applying with the Dropout layer the next layer is called Max pool 1D so what this layer does is it only keeps the strongest features if you set this value to two which you are able to do with this layer it'll take Only the strongest value for each pair of two so let's say we have this tensor of four different of four different values what it'll do is take the two first values compare those and now two is the biggest so that's the one we'll take it'll also take three and four and compare those and four is the biggest so that's what we'll take so we'll end up with the following array right here instead of this one this means it will only keep the strongest feature features and work with them instead of all the features so if we for example have an audio signal with a bunch of different fluctuations instead of keeping all the data we'll only keep the strongest Peaks because those are probably what matters so if there is silence in the audio for example that's not a very strong Peak so that will be filled out this will make processing in the Neal Network  an be necessary if we do have this timeout issue and we aren't able to optimize the endpoint enough we could also be able to support this Max duration or sorry this 60 second invocation timeout by
